ParlaMint-AT_2004-03-25-022-XXII-NRSITZ-00056_d7e3097	We're coming to the 1st. Question by Mrs Rasinger to the Federal Minister for Health and Women. – Ladies and gentlemen, please.
ParlaMint-AT_2004-03-25-022-XXII-NRSITZ-00056_d7e3113	Thank you, Mrs. Minister! My question is: 42/M “What activities do you set up to improve health promotion in Austria?”
ParlaMint-AT_2004-03-25-022-XXII-NRSITZ-00056_d7e3139	Mrs Bundesminister, please.
ParlaMint-AT_2004-03-25-022-XXII-NRSITZ-00056_d7e3155	Mr President. High house! At the beginning of this legislative period, we have undertaken to rethink health, i.e. away from exclusive medical repair thinking, instead a new additional focus and focus on health promotion. We have therefore already started last year not only with the conception, but also with the implementation of our major health promotion programme, whereby we have undertaken to apply this in target group-specific offers to as many Austrians as possible and not only to raise awareness, but above all to stimulate behavioural changes. This health promotion programme is a holistic one. It includes the areas of nutrition, exercise, stress reduction or – even better! – Stress prevention, accident prevention and what we have previously understood through classical medical care. A comprehensive health promotion movement, which is intended to reach all eight million Austrians, can only work in cooperation with many cooperation partners. We have therefore also turned target group specific and risk group specific to appropriate cooperation partners. These are, of course, the kindergartens for children and young people, the schools, the non-school youth organisations, the children's clinics. These are for the active population between 15 and 65, especially those with occupational health promotion measures. We have already been able to win thousands of businesses to take health promotion measures in their companies. They have gained the very positive experience that these investments are ultimately expected, because not only the satisfaction of employees leads to a better operating environment, but also such measures have an impact on the number of days of sick working and thus also have a direct impact on the efficiency and productivity of businesses. We have, above all, addressed the seniors to the seniors, especially to the seniors, who are cross-party, as well as, of course, to the media, especially the ORF, which has assured us to support us in the awareness-building measures. However, behavioural or lifestyle changes will only be achieved if we consistently convince the Austrians, in small steps, that healthy diet can be enjoyable, that it does not mean to give up everything, but that you – with measure and purpose! – can also deal properly with less healthy or fat foods. It seems important to me that we can animated the Austrians into more whole grain, more vegetables, more vitamins – i.e. fruit – and above all “move” them. In recent years, through the motorization that has taken place throughout Austria, we have obviously forgotten a little to set our tools in motion on a regular basis. This is both about the everyday movement and about sport. Rising up can be worthwhile, even as the health situation reaches. But that alone is too little. So: Walks and moderate sports are useful! There is a lot more action to this, but I think that would lead too far now. There may be some additional questions.

ParlaMint-AT_2004-03-25-022-XXII-NRSITZ-00056_d7e3254	As I said earlier, primarily through cooperation partners. I shall perhaps put it into practice by means of a few examples. We have just, together with the Vienna City School Council, completed a programme for teaching subjects in folk schools – the “food detectives” – with which the nutritional value of the individual foods is very consciously addressed in playful form. This programme is now being offered to all teaching staff teachers and is linked to very appealing workbooks. It is suggested that one also deals with it. The same is true for kindergartens. And above all, we will focus on the eighth level – the fourth classes of the gymnasium, the primary school and the general special school – in the course of which we will, this year already with a trial course, but hopefully next year, be very broad and from the very next year throughout all the fourth primary, secondary, special school classes, a project day or a project week on health, where these five areas of nutrition, exercise, stress prevention, accident prevention and medical precautions can be dealt with primarily in age-specific terms – for example, what the sport or moped driving is about, the accident hazards in road traffic. We will combine this with the already existing examination at the eighth level, this final examination or intermediate examination, which we have now standardized. We have, for example, the first Austrian school doctor to attend the conference on 14. January in Linz, where we have standardized this investigation and, as we are now comparable, have agreed that it will be evaluated anonymously in the ministry. This will give us good information about the health status of our 14-year-olds. Unfortunately, we have very much to fight with obesity up to obesity among children. In the case of operational initiatives, AUVA has, in a grateful way, set up an operational health promotion centre, which is particularly available for information to businesses. Large businesses have a little easier because they have a business doctor who can concentrate on it. Small and medium-sized enterprises have a little harder. Therefore, for example, the Tirol Chamber of Commerce, together with UNIQA and the ORF Tirol, has set up an operational health promotion offensive, aimed primarily at small and medium-sized enterprises and aimed at movement, nutrition, which VitalClub attracts to the enterprises. We will start this year in the federal government with an initiative “Business Health Promotion in the Ministries”. The Federal Chancellery, the Federal Ministry of Health and Women and the Ministry of Social Affairs will develop, implement and implement such a vital programme for their employees. I hope that it will succeed in getting the appropriate support for this in the public service as well. We also have, of course, risk-group-specific offers, especially with regard to the weaning of smokers; we are planning an initiative against smoking in particular for young people. Unfortunately, within Europe we keep a record that is extremely unpleasant, because in Austria, 20 percent of 15-year-old boys and even 26 percent of 15-year-old girls say they smoke regularly. This number is terrifying, especially when you add the dark figures. I think that we should make every effort to stop children and young people – the age of entry – sometimes even at 11 or 12 years of age – from making them strong so that they don't need it. The slogan is actually: “I don’t need it!” – There was already a good initiative of the “Healthy Austria” Fund.

ParlaMint-AT_2004-03-25-022-XXII-NRSITZ-00056_d7e3886	Mrs. Federal Minister! An important area is women's health. A British study has shown that hormone replacement therapy is said to have increased the risk of breast cancer. What measures have you taken in this context?
ParlaMint-AT_2004-03-25-022-XXII-NRSITZ-00056_d7e3902	Mrs Bundesminister, please.
ParlaMint-AT_2004-03-25-022-XXII-NRSITZ-00056_d7e3917	That's right, ladies and gentlemen. Last summer, this study has also aroused great excitement in Austria and, above all, has brought concern to many women who have used hormone replacement therapies in recent years. On the day of the publication of this article, I contacted Univ.-Prof. Huber, who is active in this field in Austria, and asked the Chairman of the Supreme Council of Sanity, that the Supreme Council should deal with this issue. We set up a hotline in the ministry the following day, which was used very intensively. Around 200 calls daily in the first days have shown how urgent the problem is for women. Within a period of three months, the Supreme Council of Sanity has drawn up an opinion on this subject, or a communication, which was then revised at a large congress of the hormone specialists worldwide, which took place in Vienna in November. It noted that hormone replacement therapy can increase the risk of breast cancer and that therefore hormone replacement therapy as a recommendation for women should never be given without indication, so never just prophylactically in the climatium, but only when appropriate indications are given – and then as short as possible and as small as possible. We have prepared this information and sent it to all doctors. It was printed in our house and is included in the medical journals. There are corresponding brochures or printing flags available to the doctors for the patients. The hotline in this context is no longer in demand in the meantime.

We promote initiatives that strengthen the self-confidence of women who support the self-protection or self-defense of women, which is of course not entirely irrelevant in the public sphere, and which, above all, aim to empower women – the word “empowerment” has become very important in the women’s movement – to make free decisions about their lives and to shape their lives accordingly. In other words, medications are mainly tested during the test phase when not exclusively on male test subjects, because their organism is somewhat more structured and there is no objection to hormonal fluctuations in the course of the women's cycle, because there is a lower risk that the man becomes pregnant during the test period, which is still the main reason why some women do not take them, and because, obviously, the medicine is also sexually blind in many cases. There are some internationally proven models where it is said, but it is important that medications and their effect on women are equally tested, because it has been found that medications, as simple as aspirin, can have different effects on women and men, because often the doses for women, but also for children, are misstated. That is to say, it is clearly necessary to test its effects on men, women and children in the testing phase of a new drug, which is not going on today. I did not even ask the Minister of the Budget in the Committee on Budgets a year ago how she handled this in Austria, and at that time I had the impression of finding out that the question was going to be put to the test and that hormone preparations such as the antibaby pill were already being tested on women. Now I'm pretty calm. However, what does not happen is that, in Austria, one looks specifically at the fact that all medications have also been tested on women. In the meantime, at least a certain learning process has been used. In the Committee – I think it was a week ago – Mrs. Minister pointed out that, in the meantime, large companies in the pharmaceutical testing phase, women are usually admitted to the sample at a certain percentage. To rely on the fact that this happens by myself is too little for me. I would therefore like to make sure that we in Austria pay particular attention only to allowing medications or even to promote pharmacological studies if both men and women are tested. Here we could take an example of the National Institutes of Health in the USA, which only promote pharmacology studies if men and women are involved. I should therefore like to make the following request and ask the Minister in this direction: [[Quoted print version removed]] Mrs Minister, I urge you, as Minister of Health, to take up your role as Minister of Women as Minister of Health. They can enter into productive negotiations with themselves and do not have to contact another partner and overcome their resistance. You have to overcome your own resistance, but in the overcome of pigs' dogs inside and outside, you are certainly tested. I therefore hope for your support. Thank you.

For example, the Federal Ministry of Health and Women has to prohibit the conduct of clinical trials within 35 days if the application is not the guideline of ethics. Commission agrees whether the relevance of the clinical trial is dubious. If the place of residence is not within that period, the application shall be deemed approved. In recent years, the number of clinical trials in Austria has increased. During 1997, approximately 2,500 patients participated in a clinical study, 264 studies were conducted in 2001 with a total of 6 300 patients, and 280 studies in 2002 were already in 6 500 patients. However, I would now like to focus on a subject which the Federal Minister has already addressed, namely the Committee on Health, Public Health and Consumer Health, which is particularly concerned with the St. Anna Kinderspital and the paediatric hematoecology in Austria. I quote from this observation: “The Health Committee assumes that the provisions of § 2a para. 1 the definition of clinical examination is understood to be such that, as before, individual medical trials are the necessary part of the therapy... and in which the individual benefit for the patient is so superior to the gain of medical knowledge beyond the individual case so clearly that a medical research project cannot be referred to, does not fall within the concept of clinical examination as defined in this provision." I can also support this from my personal experience with St. Anna and will now go into it shortly. Since most of the oncological medicinal products are not specifically authorised for children and often only for limited age groups, the treatment of children suffering from cancer is in most cases carried out in the context of therapy optimization studies based on the experience and developments of the last 30 years. Children who previously had no real chance of survival can expect long-term survival rates of over 80 percent for leukemia and 60 to 70 percent for solid tumours due to these long-term and proven cooperative treatment strategies. What is the problem with the clinical trial assignment? – The reason for this is the multiple conditions, not least because of the necessary probation insurance and the need for a sponsor in addition to the in-patient patient liability insurance. Therefore, therapy optimization studies could no longer be conducted, since both the necessary money and the sponsor are not available in most cases. This situation means that many children who suffer from life-threatening cancers can no longer be treated therapeuticly. It is extremely difficult to find funding for the further development of a substance in critical diseases with poor forecasting. From the point of view of St. Anna, these therapy optimization studies do not focus on the collection of scientific data, but rather on the therapy of a single child and thus, in the truest sense of the word, the life of a single child. If it were actually a clinical trial in the present case, the consequence would be that because of the lack of financial resources many children could no longer be treated according to international standards. – So much for reasons. This will bring about the simplification of administrative rules and the introduction of transparent procedures in this Federal Act on the amendment of the Medicines Act. In view of the shortened deadlines, clinical trials may be carried out in: Austria started earlier than in other Member States of the Community. This gives rise to a competitive advantage and positive employment policy impulses. In many other areas, the government, under Chancellor Dr. Wolfgang Schüssel, also sets measures, such as the tax reform, to secure and expand Austria's location for the economy and employees.

In the course of the amendment of the Medicines Act, it is also possible to control food supplements containing doping agents and therefore to qualify as medicines in the distribution areas such as sports stores and supermarkets and, in any case, to be seized. This will close a gap in the control of doping agents, because the rules on the control of doping in sport in 2001 relate only to areas of clubs dedicated to the exercise of sport or to the promotion of health or fitness, or where sporting events or competitions take place. In the future, this new task will also be be transferred by the Head of State and are operated by food supervisory bodies in order not to impose additional administrative burden here. The possibility of confiscation is intended for justified suspicions. This means that there will normally already be an examination of a sample. The bodies which are particularly trained should also be the sampling of food supplements suspected of being medicinal products on the basis of their objective or subjective purpose. Sampling will be carried out within the scope of the sample and revision plan set out by the health sector in accordance with § 36 Food Law. From a technical point of view, it should be assumed that only a small proportion of all food supplements are contained in substances according to § 5a pharmacovigilance law, i.e. only in relevant sportsman products. An investigation under which 72 relevant products were examined has shown that only three products contained prohibited substances of which only one product contained prohibited substances in doping relevant quantities. Today, we are indeed improving essential rules to combat customs fraud and also excise fraud. It is true that customs duties are losing their importance due to the increasing liberalisation of trade, and the share of EU budget financing is constantly falling, but the issue of cross-border crime has become increasingly important. It is about becoming more efficient, and it is about improving the powers of our customs authorities. The aim is to regulate how observation is to be carried out, how information is collected, how follow-up measures, including cross-border follow-up and cooperation, how images can be taken at certain places, and, in particular, how money laundering, money transport, cash transport, the transport of securities-holders' papers can be countered. Today, we will decide on many valuable regulations which we have not had so far and which are aimed at improving our safety overall. It is important that this law is based on the provisions of the Security Police Act and that citizens' rights are highly protected to the extent necessary and in our case. It is, however, a matter of combating money from criminal origin, criminal machinations, and of discovering such machinations before including them in the economic cycle, such proceeds of money. We have provided for practical provisions in the law which are based on the latest state of safety technology, and we have also introduced mutual information obligations in order to avoid duplication. It is indeed true – and this is right and important – that the guard body is strengthened by the customs guard organs and is located in the field of the Ministry of the Interior, because it creates a uniform security guard for all these areas, ranging from drug crime to international money. always has the same topic and the same customers and therefore must also work with appropriate efficiency. It is therefore the right decision of our government to propose that we vote in favour of this law, and I would like to ask the Social Democrats to put security on party policy and to agree to our proposal. ParlaMint-AT_2004-03-25-022-XXII-NRSITZ-00056_d7e8354	Mr President. Ladies and gentlemen! Mr State Secretary! There are different points under discussion. I would like to address the issue of tax harmonisation at EU level, because the item on the agenda 7 is ultimately related to this. It is a small point from the measure itself, but with great effect. The question of source taxation, the question of the taxation of income from capital is already a central issue of taxation at national level. If we now see this in the light of the fact that this type of taxation is very vulnerable to, as we believe, negative tax competitions between countries, it is all the more important that the present provisions at least indirectly bring about harmonisation. The margin for our Parliament is, in any case, not very large, because we are essentially following a directive. But I want to mention it anyway, because that is really the question worth discussing. We have already had the details in the Committee. They would probably only be bored at the plenary session, but the Green Group's approval is exactly derived from this title, which is finally to be put back, albeit smaller, towards harmonisation and harmonisation. Why is this such a problem with tax competition down? – I cannot entirely follow some newspaper commentators who constantly write, everything is in competition, so are the countries, the economic locations to each other. That is true in a certain way, but there is something like a ruinous competition, and there is basically something like a prison ilemma in such situations. What's that? – This is the case, for example, if any participant in a particular decision of his own and his own optimization actually wanted to choose the variant A, but he is forced to choose the variant B because he must assume that everyone else behaves similarly. If he were to choose variant A, he would be at the end of the fool. This is a classic economic problem. In the meantime, the internationalization of the economy with this phenomenon has led us to be inter-state. This is why it is so important for us to focus on central issues of taxation – through the categories one can agree – on greater harmonisation efforts. This is exactly where it is about capital, the stock of capital, but only about income from capital, because, unlike many other factors of production in the economy, capital is now very, very mobile, as one might technically say. But that does not mean anything good in the final result, as I mean, and in so far as I disagree with this mood, which sometimes prevails, a tax competition between states is also a good thing. It must, of course, be determined at national level, but also at EU level: What are the tasks of the public, the state, and how do we finance this? ParlaMint-AT_2004-03-25-022-XXII-NRSITZ-00056_d7e8521	Mr President. Mr State Secretary! Ladies and gentlemen! A few short words about the EU source tax law, to which Mr Kogler has already carried out a lot. I also believe that this is indeed one of those harmonisations that should have taken place much earlier, even in an area where we have far too little harmonisation at European level. However, even in the case of this measure, the materials still point out that the objective is, among other things, to maintain the competitiveness of EU financial markets. This is, in itself, a supportive goal, which we should not reject by hand, but it must certainly not be a self-interest. It should not be overlooked that the EU Directive on capital movements, which has already liberalised the EU's capital movement, also provides for harmonisation in the field of capital taxation. Decided issues such as tax evasion, tax evasion, tax evasion were addressed, ultimately certainly also tax competition, but – by the way – certainly not tax amnesty. This directive, ladies and gentlemen, was adopted in 1988. That is, 16 years later, we are still discussing such harmonisation measures in the EU, so I believe that it is essential to strengthen, strengthen and really address this discussion process, because otherwise it would necessarily be the point that Kogler addressed with “ruinous tax competition”. This would necessarily result from capital movements liberalisation if not countered here. It doesn't matter if we see this under the title of a Tobin tax or under the title "Tax of Tax-Oases-Transaction-Tax", which Naussnigg has introduced into the scientific discussion, at first – in order to strengthen the discussion process. That's not the point. The specific level is also not the point, but the point is to finally address this issue and to make it clear at EU level that a regulation is also needed for the competitiveness of the EU market, including with regard to the Lisbon process. Even the IMF, which, I believe, has not yet joined the Socialist International, has finally found that such harmonisations are already cheaper in the medium term than monetary policy intervention measures. Thus, the EU source tax law: A first important step, a small step, further steps must be taken quickly. Thank you.

ParlaMint-AT_2004-03-25-022-XXII-NRSITZ-00056_d7e8585	Mr President. Mr State Secretary! Ladies and gentlemen! High house! Even if Mr Kogler said that if we were to get closer to this EU source tax law, it would all be boring – it's only half there anyway – and perhaps the rest would fall asleep, then perhaps before lunch everyone would be awake. ==References== Last June, EU finance ministers agreed on this EU interest rate tax directive. The aim of this directive, of course, we have already heard, is to avoid tax relief or effective taxation of interest income in the Member State concerned. We already have this in the case of sales tax, and I believe that it is very necessary for capital income to be real for the Member State to be taxed. The application of this Directive is limited to interest income from claims, such as cash deposits or bonds, and is to be transposed into national law by the end of 2004. This is what we have done – subject to the agreement of all EU finance ministers, of course, if all tax havens, i.e. Switzerland and Liechtenstein, also take the corresponding measures, otherwise it would be “net” for these: Within the EU, there are then such measures, such regulations and this EU interest rate directive, but outside, in tax havens, of course, it continued as before. In addition to Luxembourg and Belgium, Austria is allowed to charge this withholding tax from 1. In January 2005, we shall not have to forward control reports to foreign financial authorities. In detail, in addition to many other things, the law also defines the provision against double taxation. This means that Austrian investors are not at all affected by double taxation and that all others resident in one Member State can be charged with the withholding tax. Of course, a refund may also be made if this tax is lower in the Member State concerned. In my opinion, this law now makes it really clear that tax is taxed in the Member State concerned; it is also another instrument for preventing tax evasion and combating tax evasion, despite the maintenance of our Austrian banking secret. In this context, it is also welcome that all four parties agree and have decided unanimously in the Financial Committee.

ParlaMint-AT_2004-03-25-022-XXII-NRSITZ-00056_d7e8766	President. — I call the Group of the European People's Party (Christian Democrats). - Please.
ParlaMint-AT_2004-03-25-022-XXII-NRSITZ-00056_d7e8786	Mr President. High house! In the context of the new customs guard system, I would like to note that the customs organization is divided into two areas: Part of the tasks, especially customs duties, have always been carried out by civil servants; the second part of the task, that of the customs guard, was originally the one to guard the “green border”, so it was also an executive guard. [[Abg. Grandruck: That's it!]] EU accession was announced in 1995. At the time, as a result of EU accession, customs duties for customs guards had changed in quantitative terms, since a large part of the external border did not provide for security as a green border. At the time, we had 7 000 customs guards who were systematically dismantled, transferred to other areas, to other federal states, especially in the East and South. This stock of 7 000 customs guards has been reduced as a result of and due to EU accession to the last 2 600. There is now again a crucial date in relation to the EU, namely the enlargement of the EU towards the East. Austria will then have no EU external borders until Switzerland, Liechtenstein, it will become almost an EU country. Therefore, quantitative tasks which have so far been carried out by the customs guard are withdrawn. On the basis of this change due to the external constellation, the decision was to make how the current customs guard is used or how the future task of the customs guard should be, if there is almost no “green border” – there is only a very modest one, as has been said, to Switzerland and Liechtenstein. We have spent months working on different concepts for this. The last, current regulation was the one that included part of the customs guard into the civil customs guard – creating its own service regulations so that no loss of salary can be found, so that even higher-quality posts can be found for it – the other part, since the scope of the task is smaller, transfer to the Gendarmerie security guard, police, because in any case more personnel is needed due to a greater safety need, so that 1 030 staff remain with us and 1 030 staff are transferred to the Ministry of the Interior. To this end, in order to be able to proceed largely in accordance with the voluntary principle, we have informed about the situation. We have informed each employee of the new areas of responsibility which will result from the locations and offered them the opportunity to choose freely, either to opt for the Ministry of Finance or to opt for the interior service. Although a total of 2 000 staff were addressed and we did not actually know how the staff themselves will decide, the option has been dealt with relatively precisely. In the end, only 80 staff have opted for the Ministry of the Interior more than the number 1 030. We can therefore largely respond to the concerns of the staff, and where they have opted for them, they will also come. [[Abg. May. Worm: How many have retired?]] Therefore, most of the staff in the new job and its evaluation are completely clear. There has been very good cooperation with the Ministry of the Interior, also trade unions, personnel representatives have been involved in this selection, and it has worked very well. In this context, we had a problem area, that is, Vorarlberg, because the option ratio was not in line with the actual figures as regards the need – and in addition, there had already been a mixed organization between customs guard and the Ministry of the Interior in the security of the external border or the occupation of customs posts. If, as it were, we had made a clean breakdown between the Ministry of the Interior and the Ministry of Finance, there would have been a need for more personnel. Therefore, in Vorarlberg we hand over the entire customs guard to the Ministry of the Interior, but with the obligation that the Ministry of the Interior should take over the remaining customs duties in Vorarlberg; that was the compromise. On the basis of this regulation, which has been adopted in the last few days in a Convention, very few posts of service are still open. Where these come from, as this will be regulated, will certainly be given a solution in the next few days. To this end, talks with the Central Committee on Customs Guard will be held next Friday – under my leadership. Thank you.

(Parliament adopted the legislative resolution) Previously, Mr Maier – which seems to have been a really remarkable event – complained that the conference center was not built with Voest-Stahl in 1982. Austria, however, joined the EC Customs Agreement at the beginning of the 1970s after Bruno Kreisky founded a single government. From this point on, it was no longer possible to maintain these protectionist tariffs in Austria, which, at least, did not have a major obstacle to the performance of the Austrian economy. This, of course, has led to major problems in some farms at the time. I'm just thinking of Steyr-Daimler-Puch in the truck and tractor area. Or it also led to the Voest no longer having the monopoly to sell steel exclusively in Austria because others could not deliver. We have gone a very wide way in these 35 years. Austria is now continuing to ratify this agreement as the next step in the WTO, which the candidate countries will also participate in. In addition, Russia will also take this step within the WTO. This is also a very important moment in this matter of law. If 40 of the approximately 60 partners in the respective national parliaments have been ratified, this agreement will therefore enter into force. I believe that this is a good thing in the final effect, because the reduction of these protective duties, which, especially in the reform states, also prevent it from challenging the performance of its own economy, will lead to more competition, more productivity and more efficiency, as was the case in Austria at that time. In total, this is a step in the right direction. We Social Democrats will also agree.

ParlaMint-AT_2004-03-25-022-XXII-NRSITZ-00056_d7e9645	Mr President. Mr State Secretary! Ladies and gentlemen! I refer to the agreement between the Republic of Austria and the United Arab Emirates. Of course, such an agreement, which lays down rules for tax benefits, makes sense in principle. However, I would like to take this opportunity to say a few words in total about this bilateral agreement. We Greens strongly criticise the WTO World Trade Organisation on the basis of its undemocratic rules of play and also on the basis of its mechanisms which contribute to a disparity between the poor developing countries and the rich countries of the North. Nevertheless, it must be seen that in many cases a multilateral agreement is preferable to a bilateral agreement and that the multilateral agreements simply have certain advantages. If, by the example of this agreement between Austria and the United Arab Emirates, one now imagines that at EU level each country has such a bilateral agreement within the EU, one can also imagine that the tax jungle is more compacted than enlightened by such agreements. The effort to look at each of the tax benefits is then quite large. That means: From our point of view, it would be useful not only to harmonise taxes overall at EU level, but also to take a step in the area of these agreements between countries and the EU, so that at least there is a common procedure at EU level and that anger can be reduced in bilateral agreements. Similar complex links exist in the bilateral investment protection agreements. We are pleased that the government fractions are ready to organise a survey on this subject at the request of the opposition. Although it is a difficult and complex matter, we believe that it is necessary once to discuss how our ministers behave at EU level in the field of finance and the economy. We are pleased, as has been said, that this also finds support for the government groups, and we hope that we can discuss the position of Austria at EU level within the framework of this survey.

ParlaMint-AT_2004-03-25-022-XXII-NRSITZ-00056_d7e9830	We now reach the 14th. Agenda item. There was no oral reporting. First speaker, Mr Matznetter. - Please.
ParlaMint-AT_2004-03-25-022-XXII-NRSITZ-00056_d7e9854	Mr President. Mr State Secretary! Ladies and gentlemen! Today's proposal has a long history, in which the waves have already gone in one direction and the other; last with an "notary operation" one hour before we have voted here. What is it all about? – At the heart of the issue is the implementation of the Seeling case, a case in which the ECJ – in principle not unsurprisingly – has its jurisdiction in the past. Following the EC Directive, when assessing the self-consumption of a mixed land, the trader has granted it the opportunity, on the one hand, to carry VAT as his own consumption, but on the other hand, with a deduction of the costs of the VAT on construction costs and other expenditure, in the context of his accounting system as a trader. There would have been two answers: The answer would have been to find a solution that fully implements the judgment. However, we should have implemented a regulation that in the case of a property it is no longer an advantage to have a full pre-tax deduction and then only ten years later, under non-R, to be able to return the property to other purposes. Instead, it is intended to put in place a measure to remedy the situation by means of a possible derogation provided for in Article 6(2) of the 6. Using the EC directive, which we already know will not keep. Consequently, it is also planned to establish a safety net with this change. This is to be done through the introduction of the stock of revenue with the 20-year pre-tax correction in the event that the company incorporates into the accounting the partially privately used property with pre-tax deduction, using the ECJ's jurisdiction in the field of sailing. I do not think that such a law-making technique makes sense for two reasons: First: The more Austrian law is repealed by the ECJ, the more we raise the outward appearance that we are unable to implement the EU directives properly. Secondly, we are setting a second blow by introducing the 20-year rule only for a part, but otherwise leave the ten-year rule. The question of inequality is therefore the next point of this provision. In this context, it would have been good to consider why a pre-tax deduction in a building actually brings an economic advantage to the company. – We all know why it is: So the many rental models, where you buy, create a construction in which you rent, opt for VAT at business premises or even better – even better! – spent with 10 percent in living rooms and transferred the property after ten years to the person who was previously the user, who then ultimately had to bear a lower tax than one who bought a property or rented a cooperative apartment. It doesn't taste to us! We want equal treatment here, even if we know that there are cases of problem. It would have been better to find a general solution and then perhaps in the business area where hardships would have been, only to improve them. That is why we do not want to give this part of the vote. I ask you to understand that. Thank you.

ParlaMint-AT_2004-03-25-022-XXII-NRSITZ-00056_d7e10741	Mr Secretary of State Dr. Finz. - Please.
ParlaMint-AT_2004-03-25-022-XXII-NRSITZ-00056_d7e10759	Mr President. High house! The establishment of a accounting agency – by the way not as a central institution, but as a body in Vienna with three offices distributed among the regions in Austria – is currently the largest administrative reform project. Why? – The division of resorts has proved to be extremely detrimental in this area. In fact, similar tasks are currently being carried out – proof processing in the State Defense Ministry or the Ministry of Education or the Ministry of the Interior – according to the location principle, there are 32 accounting records. The problem is that it is not possible to comply with the optimum size of the operation due to the budgetary rules, because compliance with the incompatibility rules requires at least seven people, regardless of whether I really need it after the quantitative income. That is why we have said, for many years, that disadvantage: If we now have a modern automated accounting system, with a final and truly modern software, that is, the product SAP in conjunction with the ELAK, i.e. the electronic act, which will be available from the autumn of this accounting agency, so that no evidence will have to be transmitted – that is, Mr Matznetter, unfortunately, he is not there, there is no evidence to be sent, they remain in the stores, it is practically a fully automated procedure without written evidence – this is clearly beneficial. We are consciously starting from 2001 on the basis of the assessment. At that time, we still had over 1,000 services, distributed among the various accounts, universities, which were formally included at that time, otherwise it would be 1 500 staff at all. We did not consciously reoccupy anymore because we knew we were setting up a accounting agency. We didn't want to be stupid about taking up staff if we had the goal that by the end of 2010, only half of the staff should be employed. Then why did the accounting company work anyway? . We have focused only more on the core business of accounting, on the actual accounting and control, and so-called administrative assistance services that can but do not have to do – for example, it can carry out tendering documents or set up statistics for any regional situation – to the most necessary extent. As a result, it has been possible to work with fewer staff in the organization currently in place. But now the border is reached. We enter the accounting agency with 685 staff and will only have more than 500 staff in the final development – that will be until 2007/2008. The savings between 2004 and 2006 alone will amount to €31 million. Show me, please, a project where you can save €31 million. I think these figures can be seen, I can also prove them in detail. So there will be no evidence, Mr Matznetter, there will be no great central authority, because these future 500 staff will be divided across Austria, it will be a simple long-distance booking procedure. The accounting agency will fulfil the real purposes of accounting, namely the control of compliance with budgetary rules. In the future, the payment obligations will be triggered at the right time with a modern software. We have the problem today that the federal government pays either too early or too late. It's too early to say there's a loss of interest. If he pays too late, he can't take advantage of Skonti. As far as the appointment of management is concerned, I would like to say that: No managing director has yet been appointed, only on the basis of a selection of directors is planned, but because we do not yet have the legal basis, we have been waiting for the signing of the contract. Furthermore, the evaluation of disassembly has shown that one should already select personnel or future managing directors at the preparatory stage, because they should cooperate in the organisation, in the establishment of the accounting agency, in the organisational, structural measures and the like, in the selection of which officials are to be ordered. The Federal Ministry Act has also institutionalized the planned administration for this purpose, which is also legally possible. If we were to interpret the principle of legality as strictly as you did today, Mr Kogler, then we could not even in future develop a draft law for any matter, because it does not comply with the principle of legality. We therefore also advise, in particular, in the Convention Committee on 6 on a further relaxation of the principle of legality, because flexibility, the rapid transition to new circumstances, an important point, is also important for the administration today. We can't go with an administration of the 19th. ==References====External links== ==References== [[Congratulations on the ÖVP and the Freeman.]] To the Court's opinion, I would just like to point out that the Court of Auditors was closely involved in this project because, in accordance with § 6 of the Court of Auditors' Act, the order of accounting is the Ministry of Finance's responsibility together with the Court of Auditors. If the Court of Auditors had said no, we could not have created this accounting agency law. We have therefore received the explicit consent of the Court of Auditors to this law. And today – I still have the opportunity to sit here on the government bank with Mr President-in-Office – you can obtain this approval. The §-6 procedure is a separate procedure for the assessment process. After all, it was only a matter of concern whether the ELAK is coming in time – and I assure you that the ELAK is coming in time. We have received formal consent in accordance with § 6 of the Court of Auditors Act. - Thank you.

The aim of the 2003 reform was therefore to stabilise these rising trends at a low level. On the other hand, gentle and socially balanced reforms in the pension sector require a very long lead period until they are fully effective. Therefore, they must be started as early as possible in the sense of a long-term guarantee of the pension system. Due to the transitional periods, there can be no mention of “drastic instant cuts”. On question 2: It should be noted that net losses are moving in the requested area. A final statement is not possible at present, as hardly any persons have retired in accordance with the new legal situation. A final assessment will be possible after the end of 2004. However, from my point of view, it should be noted that a non-insignificant number of women are currently benefiting from the new legal situation, especially when using early retirement pensions for long periods of insurance. In addition to the comments made on this question, the following can be said: It is expected that around 10 percent of the new additions to 2004 will not experience any reductions or even an increase in their pensions. Dr. Niederwieser: But that was no answer!]] Question 4: It must be noted that the number of years of insurance does not state how long someone has actually worked. The Federal Government has always stressed that long-term employment is also to be rewarded. The pension reform assumes that male insured persons with 45 years of insurance are completed at 65. A pension of 80 per cent of the basis of assessment is entitled to a life-year pension. In the event of a previous retirement, a discount must be expected. In contrast to the “hackler scheme” for normal early retirement pension of 65. Year of life calculated. If there are no more than 45 years of insurance, the discount is calculated and deducted by a maximum of 80 percent. For persons who have acquired many years of contributions – i.e. long worked, at least 45 years of contributions, including certain replacement periods – the discount is only expected by the individually raised age of seizure for early retirement. If there are more than 45 years of insurance, there is not even an 80 percent cover, but the long-term activity will also be positive in the bill. Question 5: “What reason do you find for the fact that men with 45 years of contribution can be covered by the “protection” regulations that you strongly promote for so-called “hacklers” and still retire at 60, but also receive 10 percent reductions in their pension?” [[Abg. Dr. Niederwieser: You don't need to read questions!]] Ladies and gentlemen! Generally speaking, it must be said that women benefit more from the long-term scheme than men. This is due to the fact that women have rarely reached more than 80 percent of the amount of increase according to the old legal situation. The advantage of the “hackler regulation” is, rather, that an earlier use of pensions is still possible and that the discount is calculated not by the age of the rule of retirement, but by the individual age of early retirement. Certain replacement times – the times of child rearing and the times of military service – are now valued as contribution times. On question 6: The amount of pension, where a benefit from the hardness fund is possible, depends on the minimum social security for married couples – that is €1 015. This figure has been increased by more than 20 percent since 1990. At our government start in 2000, the average pension was just €708 in the entire statutory pension insurance. Pensions of 1 015 € are therefore, unfortunately, located in the top third. Question 7: The pension adjustments for the years 2004 and 2005 are likely to provide for a value-reserving pension adjustment for all pensions up to the level of the median pension. The fact that the above-mentioned pensions receive only a fixed amount of around €10 per month is to be seen as a solidarity contribution of this group to the overall package of pension reform 2003. From 2006 onwards, all pensions will be adjusted to the full inflation rate – a scheme required by all pensioners’ organisations. A withdrawal of the pension reform would continue to perpetuate the miserable system of net adjustment which has not yet been properly understood. Question 8: The statement on this question is not correct. In the context of the long-term scenarios prepared for the Round Table for Pension Harmonisation, there are very likely to be calculations that bring a much greater increase in pension expenses. Question 9: Yes. As a result of the premise that each contribution euro should lead to the same pension benefit, of course, various scenarios have been calculated. I would like to stress that some 70 percent of the calculations for harmonisation have been made on the basis of the wishes of the workers' representatives in the negotiating team, the Chamber of Labour and the ÖGB. Question 10: Since I do not know what assumptions are behind this statement by the Federal Chancellor, I will not comment on it either. On the question 11: I have already publicly presented my idea of pension harmonisation several times. As you know, I have, among other things, expressed my support for the transitional right of establishment. It is indisputable that benefits with 45 years of insurance are 65 years of age. 80 per cent of the basis of assessment is intended for the age of 80. In addition, about 50 percent of the issues discussed by experts as key points are already agreed at expert level. I do not think it makes sense for individual groups to represent their ideas and opinions before the negotiations as the only true thing, to cement themselves with them and to make it more difficult to negotiate. I should like to make every effort to ensure that the harmonisation, which is urgently required by the general public, is resolved as soon as possible, comprehensively and consistently as possible with the social partners at the negotiating table. Question 12: This question can only be assessed with a view to the conclusion of the negotiations. In any case, the 10-percent cover is already valid for not covered by statutory pension insurance. On the question 13: Although I answered question 9 with Yes, I would like to make it clear that I am not thinking of withdrawing the 2003 pension reform. This would primarily be significantly at the expense of the younger generation and in 2010 generate an additional need and a need for multi-negotiation of around €2 billion at the expense of the younger generation. ParlaMint-AT_2004-03-25-022-XXII-NRSITZ-00056_d7e16915	Mr President. I'll do my best. - High house! Although there are a number of measures to be taken in recent weeks and months, Mr Pirklhuber, this discussion of an answer to a question offers me the opportunity to provide you with all the information you miss in the public, namely the fact that something is happening and that, in accordance with the views of the Austrians for a non-genotechnic Austria, it is also traded and lobbying within the European Union. Austria is still, and I think quite rightly, taking a very critical attitude to genetic engineering in feed and food production. In December 2003, Austria, together with Denmark, Greece, France, Luxembourg and Portugal, opposed the authorisation of Bt-11 maize as genetically modified Food was voted, while Belgium, Germany and Italy abstained. Unfortunately, this voting behaviour clearly shows that maintaining the moratorium will be difficult. In order to ensure the maintenance of the moratorium, the Minister of Agriculture Dipl.-Ing. Pröll a letter to all environmental and agricultural ministers, in which he drew attention to the still unresolved issues of risk assessment and co-existence. The aim was, above all, to make the opposition of Austria to genetic engineering in food understandable to the other ministers and to achieve a continuation of the moratorium. To strengthen the Austrian position, a research project on “toxicology and allergology of GMO products” was commissioned by my house. The aim of this research project was to analyse EU-wide product applications for genetically modified plants and food and to draw up recommendations for the standardisation of the safety assessment. As a study recommendation, it was therefore noted that in addition to acute toxicity and allergenity, other end products such as chronic toxicity, carcinogenicity and reproductive toxicity should be identified in the safety assessment. This could also detect any secondary harmful effects of gene modification. The objections of Austria, including the Bt-11 maize, are based on the findings of this study. A personal letter from me to Commissioner for the Environment Wallström was also attached to the current study to support the objections of Austria. The dossier on Bt-11 maize is expected to be published on 27 May. Adopted by the Council of Agriculture Ministers on 4 April 2004. We will do everything we can to ensure that the qualified majority for approval is not achieved. However, even if there is no qualified majority for authorisation, the Commission could still approve this product unanimously. The Commission's approval would mean that the moratorium will be finally terminated. The Commission had previously repeatedly asked Member States to abolish national bans against genetically modified food and feed, since from its point of view with effect from 18. The legislative package on genetically modified organisms to be applied in the EU in April 2004 provides conditions for the end of the moratorium. The two EU regulations on traceability and labelling ensure a high level of environmental and health protection across Europe. These provisions also allow for a uniform approach for the traceability of GMOs from the field to the plate. In my section, the debate on necessary measures and the legal basis for the implementation of European genetic engineering regulations has been held intensively since the spring of last year. You have already mentioned it, Mr Deputies. Already before the summer, a genetic engineering law novelle was sent to implement the Release Directive in review. It was my intention to introduce this law to the Council of Ministers in November 2003. By the Federal Ministry of Education, Science and Culture as well as by the objections of the Federal Ministry of Economic Affairs and Labour, the new law was again subject to an interministerial discussion process. At the meeting of the Commission on Genetic Engineering in February of this year, I also put the draft law to the discussion. Not only in the Committee of Genetics, but also in a group of experts led by the Ministry of Agriculture and the Environment, who have taken part in the initiative of the national agrarian conference, will draw up recommendations for a national strategy in order to ensure a uniform approach by the Federal Government and countries on the issue of co-existence. The labelling of genetically modified foods is therefore of great importance because it ensures the basis for maintaining freedom of choice for consumers. Given the European development of genetic engineering legislation, I have commissioned me to produce an information brochure for consumers, which explains the importance of the labels. I was particularly pleased with the commitment of the food industry to participate actively in this information as well. As the Federal Minister for Health and Women, I am also responsible for the controls you have referred to. I have only just given my opinion in reply to Parliament's question 1483/J. But already in recent years, Mr Pirklhuber, I – I did last year, but my predecessors, earlier – have caused inspections and controls on genetically modified food throughout the country. For this purpose, the food supervisory bodies of the countries have carried out sampling at production and processing establishments and in food trade. The analytical studies were carried out by the Austrian Agency for Health and Food Safety. The health resort has provided official research results on genetically modified foods on its online home page in the field of food under the item “New Foods”, which are also visible on our homepage. In summary, I can tell you that in 2000 a little more than 600 samples were taken. Of these, 2.5 percent were affected. In 2001, 4.6% were concerned, and 4.8% in 2002. The results of 2003 have not yet been fully achieved, but the trend towards the rate of opposition, which is rising, remains the same. In the framework of the 2004 priority action, which I took on 4th. In the field of genetically modified foods, the study and verification of the labelling of soybeans, maize and products derived from it were carried out on March this year. This priority action also includes organic products. The number of inspections with or without sampling has been established on the basis of the experience of the priority actions and studies in recent years and the AGES' research capacity of 345. In comparison, this figure was previously 300 per year. ==References== April 2004 includes the labelling requirement of foods containing, consisting of, or produced from genetically modified organisms, regardless of analytical evidence of genetic modification. In addition to the already mentioned sampling and analysis, documentation-related product controls are increasingly important. To be understood here is the over- the testing of company documents on the traceability of critical components, such as soya products or maize products for highly processed products, where analytical evidence is no longer possible. As guidance for the Austrian food producers, a Codex Commission sub-committee prepared a guide to ensuring traceability and was also adopted in a timely manner in the Codex Commission's plenary session yesterday. So he's ready. You, honourable Members, have either chosen a good time or I have been lucky – just as you want to interpret it. In summary, let me say that not only the companies but also the control authorities are well prepared to meet the new requirements. The customer or the customer also has the opportunity to influence the development of the food sector through appropriate information from the consumer and consumer sector. As far as the legislation on genetic engineering is concerned, I can also tell you that the negotiations are in the final phase and that we will bring this novelle in on a timely basis.

This report is a critical report from the Court of Auditors' point of view, but it does not detect any serious shortcomings. There are several optimization proposals, suggestions, adjustments, but also adaptations to the new requirements, taking into account EU enlargement. In concrete terms, this report identified three main issues: There was no standardized reporting between the organizations federal police, federal army, customs guard, federal army and district administration authorities. This report system has now been optimised by the establishment of the ISIM, the information system for illegal migration, and in the meantime it is possible to balance internal data. Criticized information losses could therefore be reduced. Secondly, there was the Court of Auditors' suggestion that, in order to fulfil security-related tasks, customs guard officials should be taken over to the Federal Ministry of the Interior. There were 1st. On September 1, 2003, 100 staff were taken over, and with 1. In May 2004, another 930 staff will be transferred. The Court's third suggestion concerns a separate cost and performance calculation for the Federal Army. – This will be introduced as a sample starting with the second quarter of 2004. In addition, Federal Minister Strasser has clearly stated in the Committee that the district authorities will continue to play a central role in the field of security in the future, that there will be no disbandment of the district command and that there will probably be further improvements in the operational field of security through the merger of police and gendarmerie. [[Congratulations on the ÖVP.]] – So much for the chapter Border Army. Another chapter – which is unfortunately only briefly mentioned here today – in the report is the appointment and appointment of supervisory councils in the field of ÖIAG. The Court of Auditors has identified four specific shortcomings. It was the nature of the tender, it was the shortened deadlines, the low number of invited staff advisors and the lack of award criteria. In this report, the President of the Court of Auditors has praised the fact that the Court of Auditors' suggestions in consultation with the Board of Directors of the ÖIAG have been largely implemented. The stencil regulation has been aligned and adapted within the meaning of the Court of Auditors. The criticism expressed by my colleague, Herbs, in the Court of Auditors Committee, that there have been salary doublings does not appear in the Court of Auditors' report. That's what the The Court of Auditors is not at all criticised. Federal Minister Grasser has also suggested or found that the award procedures are now carried out by a separate Commission with the help of experts. The Court of Auditors' report contributes to the increased transparency and objectivity of post positions. On the other hand, it has been found very critical that it is becoming increasingly difficult to find suitable people or managers for such positions. Therefore, we should also assess this issue with a certain sensitivity and face the performance and the risk of what people do when they provide for functions in such establishments. We will continue to pay attention to every critical remark in the future. However, we are not prepared to carry out scandalisations, as they did again here today. Thank you.

And in the field of investment promotion, i.e. in the development and operation of railways, we finance 50 percent of all investments. So you see that we are doing everything possible here and that no one can blame us for not fulfilling our transport policy responsibility on this issue. [[Congratulations on the ÖVP and on representatives of the Free Trade Unions.]] It has been pointed out that, for the Austrian private railways, a successor to this law will be decided, but for the first time indefinitely. The private railways now know that they have a corresponding claim to this money and that law does not have to be renewed year after year or after three or five years. This is very important for the planning safety of these companies, especially because railway infrastructure projects usually have at least 10 years ahead. We support exactly the same as the ÖBB, according to exactly the same guidelines and criteria for public services – in recent years at least €38 million that private railways have received. In total, around €55 million has been made available annually in the last five years. In addition, there will always be five-year medium-term investment programmes which define precisely the Federal conservation aid. For this, about €17 million a year, ladies and gentlemen. So you see: What we decide today is an important law, both for dealing with the transport problems of the future and for more equality between the ÖBB State Railway and the Austrian private railways.

ParlaMint-AT_2004-03-25-022-XXII-NRSITZ-00056_d7e21984	Mr President. Ladies and gentlemen! These Europe-wide harmonizations in legal systems make sense, but the problem is that, in this European arrest warrant issue, it is not possible to unify what the offence is about, but only to make mutual extradition sound and thus bring about a legal state in which I would like to show you two concrete examples of how reprehensible it can be in itself. Concrete: An Austrian supplies a French company with spare parts. He's making these deliveries. According to Austrian law he may, according to French law is the sabotage. If you decide today on this European arrest warrant, it occurs that this Austrian citizen, who has not committed any offence under Austrian law, must be delivered to France because he no longer delivers. [[Abg. Dr. Fekter: You have not read the amendment!]] That's sabotage, and that's our problem with the definition of the offences that are cited where you have to deliver. These are our concerns, and they are based on the criticism that experts in Germany have also held. The second example, otherwise: An Irin has lived in Austria for 20 years. She is an Irish citizen, but has been living here for a very long time. It is drifting away and is thus subject to criminal penalties under Irish criminal law. Ireland can demand extradition, although the Irin has long been moving in a life association that knows another Judicatur, a Judicture as in Austria, where this is not punishable. This legal EU adaptation does not protect against these cases. The European Arrest Warrant may, under adverse circumstances, cause this Irin to be transferred to, and sentenced to, its home country for this abortion offence. That is why my colleague's plea was that in Europe we should really, in general, strive for civil and criminal harmonisation and not, on the basis of exchange and extradition, create more or less a zone in which, on the basis of unshakable terms – "sabote", "cyber crime" – is practised and door and door open. These are our concerns, and that is why we are voting against them. Thank you.
